Q2 A step with more than 6 chips: (a, rec) keep the 5 most urgent, last chip says "+N more in this step" / (b) wrap that step onto a second row (taller, folds earlier) / (c) allow narrower chips with text cut with "…". Q3 Picture size: (a, rec) always the slide shape (1920 × 1080) in the window, the PNG and the block, so one picture everywhere / (b) the block may grow taller than a slide and fold less; the window and PNG stay 16:9. Q4 Switch-over: (a, rec) automatic only, a one-line note in the window says which picture it drew / (b) also a Layout choice "Automatic / Full map / Open work" in the window (the block stays automatic). Q5 A late chip that is also waiting, and the second line: (a, rec) second line stays "experiment · Status" for late, waiting and can-start; a late-and-waiting chip shows as late and the key explains "late wins" / (b) the second line starts with the word, "late · ABC0014 · Started", cut with "…" when too long (colour-blind safe, but cuts the experiment name sooner) / (c) no second line on late chips. Also to confirm by eye (no question, a picture comes with M1-4): the two colours late `#b3124f` and waiting `#c77700`.

- [x] **Callout colours outside kit notes: BUILT 2026-10-20 (owner board 5 TC5, S4; unit tests + throwaway Obsidian 1.14.4, dark and light, not by the owner; existing vaults get it through Review update… for `vault-theme.css`).** Original note: **Callout colours outside kit notes (waits for "Theme compatibility / own theme" above; owner board 2, 2026-10-20: not a yes/no) (low; CORRECTED 2026-10-19 after the owner said callouts look right in their vault).** In kit notes (`cssclasses: vt`) the theme draws callouts as cards and colours the icon with `rgb(var(--callout-color))` (`vault-theme.css:151`), so they work. The first test note had no `vt` class, so Obsidian's own callout style read `--callout-color` directly, and an RGB triplet (`vault-theme.css:55-63`) is not a colour there since 1.13: a kit callout type (focus, lab, overdue, …) used in a note WITHOUT `cssclasses: vt` shows grey. Possible fix: `--callout-color: rgb(var(--vt-*-text))` and change line 151 to `color: var(--callout-color)`. It is a look change, so ask first. The robustness audit's high row 1 is downgraded to low.
- [ ] **Q8 mark test (2026-10-19, obsidian-check, Obsidian 1.14.4, ZotLit 2.1.4 installed, no Zotero running):**
  - `>` in the middle of a line ("Book NMR +Polymers >next tue"): no pop-up, no styling, stays plain text.
  - `>` at the start of a line: a quote. Lines typed straight after it also become quote lines (Markdown's lazy continuation) until a blank line. No code or JavaScript view seen. Quick capture strips a leading `>`.
  - ZotLit (from its code): its citation pop-up opens on `[@` by default, on any device, and on a plain `@` after a space or at a line start only when its setting "@ trigger" (`citation.at-trigger`, default off) is on. No pop-up showed here because Zotero was not running. So `@` clashes for ZotLit users who switch that setting on, and for Natural Language Dates / Date List users.
  - The owner's "code / JavaScript view" with `>` was not reproduced. Possibly a `>` at a line start turning the following lines into a quote, or a `>` inside a Templater `<% %>` tag. Ask for the exact text if it happens again.

New requirement: a to-do can also arrive without the right properties from a template error, a capture moved in from the Inbox, or a note added through the file explorer, and this applies to every feature, not just I2. So the engine must normalise any note that lands in the To-dos folder (kind, day / scheduled defaults) on create, move and rename, not only at migration.

  - Q10 DECIDED (owner, 2026-10-19, after a screenshot of their to-do with 11 properties; they want few properties in the split-pane view): the slim model.
    - `scheduled` (a date) replaces both `day` and `planned`.
    - `rolled` and `countdown` are dropped. Carried over is worked out: `scheduled` before today and not done.
    - Properties are written only when they have a value: no empty `project` / `due`, no `meeting: false`.
    - A new to-do keeps `kind`, `done`, `scheduled` (only when planned) and `priority` (only when set), plus `cssclasses`.
    - No date means the Backlog. "+ New" in a day column sets that day's date (today or later only, Q1). `done_on` is stamped on tick and removed on untick.
    - What read `day` must change: the board code, lists, the kit's Weekly review / Daily note / One-off meeting / Project hub queries (updated through Review update… or a Tidy rule with before / after), and the owner's own `day` queries (the Tidy view lists them). The built-in kanban fallback groups by a weekday formula from `scheduled`, view-only.
    - Optional later: drop `cssclasses: vt` by having the plugin style notes by `kind`.
  - Q9 DECIDED: the Tidy rule "give each card a date" is off by default.
  - Q19 DECIDED (accidental ticks): every tick from the board or the chip bar shows "Ticked '<name>'. Undo" for about 8 s, and finished cards stay in the day's "N done" list all week so they can be unticked there. Unticking keeps `scheduled`, so the card returns to its day.

A "Browse icons" command that copies the picked name, for editing by hand, was offered.
    - **How the owner makes up solutions (solution-methods feedback page, 2026-10-19; for Solution prep, I23 f):**
      - A, volumetric flask: almost never.
      - B, weigh then add solvent: common, but with a target mass of solid to aim for first; the liquid is not adjusted unless the solid is very off. Around 20 % concentrations are normal.
      - Everything is in flow, so the volume concentration should matter more than the mass concentration, but the owner and the field mostly ignore it, "unless there is an easy solution". Solvent evaporation affects it anyway.
      - C, liquid by volume: not usually, because evaporation makes syringes misbehave.
      - D, everything by mass (gravimetric): "very close to what we do".
      - F, several solutes by ratio: still used, but with the solvent made up by mass, not volume.
      - E, dilution from a stock: for acid solutions (e.g. from glacial acetic acid) to fine-tune pH. Some PISA needs pH 4, so a diluted acid is made first and then added to the main solution in place of part of the water; neat acid would overshoot.
      - G, dilution series: not used by the owner, though common practice.
      - GPC and NMR samples are made up as you go with rough concentrations and are not recorded; concentration matters little there.
    - **Acid for a target pH (acid-ph-options feedback page, owner 2026-10-19; not redesigned yet, after the I2 answers):**
      - V1 (target pH → share of the water → how to make the acid solution) is closest to what they do, but:
        - use more acid solution than water: about 50 % of the aqueous phase as acid solution, not 3 %;
        - one step only;
        - a 4-figure balance (0.1 mg);
        - add the acid first, then make up the remaining mass with water.
      - V2 (two-step dilution chain): they would not make that much.
      - V3 (from a kept acid stock): they don't keep stock solutions like this.
      - The converter (wt% ⇄ mol/kg ⇄ mol/L): not what they meant. They use Sigma's acids and bases calculator, not the molarity / molality one; look at that calculator before redesigning.
    - **Acid page round 2 (acid-ph-v2 feedback page, owner 2026-10-19):** layout A (form with numbered steps) is liked over the calc table (B):
      - It must record in the note how the solution was made.
      - The owner asked whether it can go into the note and still be edited live.
      - Problem raised: leaving a form midway to look something up loses the progress.
      - Snippet forms might open like the in-line to-do entry window (anchored where you are), possibly for all snippets: the owner wants to see and test that first.
      - The reagent fields (purity wt%, density, pKa, acid / base kind) must be held in the chemical database, so concentrated reagents are just chemical notes.
      - The owner has a PDF-to-SDS Python script that might fill chemical notes from safety data sheets: review it (location to be given by the owner; never read the real vault).
      - Proposal given in chat: insert a live block (like calc tables) whose inputs and actual masses are stored in the block itself, so nothing is lost when you leave and come back; to be shown as a feedback page.
    - **Live solution block (solution-live-block feedback page, owner 2026-10-19):**
      - L3 approved as a principle: keep what is stored in the note as plain text as possible and let the plugin do the processing.
      - L2, the live block with steps, looks good; saved as `my_work/reference_visual/solution-live-block.png`.
      - The block must not pick up the owner's own vault table styling: give it its own scoped classes, and the owner may adjust their snippets.
      - ANSWERED 2026-10-19 (owner, chat): L1 is built and then adjusted once the owner has tried it; L4 and L5 are fine as shown; L6 goes ahead behind a setting, and calc-table snippets stay plain-text calc blocks as now ("keeping as much plain text as we can is better; the calc blocks already do that").
      - SDS script: the owner will copy it into `my_work/private/` for review (not there yet on 2026-10-19).
- [ ] **Templater dependency: evaluate removing it (owner, 2026-10-19, from I23 d).** Today Templater carries:
  - Alt+S (the Insert snippet.md hotkey) and 4 user scripts (labSnippets, labForm, labPick, labMethods, plus vtAsk.js);
  - 18 kit template files with about 52 Templater tag lines (To-do, Lab Book Template, Meeting, Meeting series, One-off meeting, Project hub, New project note, New concept, New topic hub, the 7 code snippets, Insert snippet);
  - folder templates (a new note made in a folder, e.g. the board's New, runs its template);
  - Lab Kit's own code calls Templater's internals in about 200 places across src/kit (obsidian-private.ts, managed-ui, setup-check, paths, ui, tidy).
  Benefits of removing it: phones need no synced user scripts (the vt.ask error of 2026-10-14); fewer setup steps and Check setup rows; the highest-risk row of the robustness audit goes away; the store review sees no private-API use of another plugin.
  Risks: the owner's own edited templates and snippets stop running (a migration and the Tidy before / after view are needed); folder templates need a plugin replacement (Bases' own "New" in a column, and Obsidian's file-create event); every template must be rewritten as a token template or a plugin command, a large job; users who use Templater for their own things keep it anyway.
  Proposal: staged. (1) I23 d1: Alt+S becomes a Lab Kit command (Templater still runs the code snippets until I23 Phase 6). (2) A spike to list every Templater use, port one template (To-do) to a plugin command plus a token template, and measure the gap. (3) The owner decides on full removal from the spike's numbers.

  - **OWNER ANSWERS 2026-10-19 (chat):**
    - (a) The PDF goes to a separate folder, possibly several: e.g. the group's shared Google Drive folder plus another backed-up location. It may differ per user, so it becomes a setting: a list of destination folders, empty by default, desktop only.
    - (b) Asked what freezing would actually do. The PDF is the record, so an edited note is not the end of the world; it just won't match. Proposed in chat: no freeze. Closing makes the PDF(s), sets `closed_on`, and puts the hash in the footer. The note stays live, and Check setup can say "edited since closed". Waiting for confirmation.
    - (c) Hazards are not needed in the final report. Think about how cloning a frozen experiment works: moot if there is no freeze.
    - (e) Phone: sensible (PDF pending, made on a computer).
    - (f) The demo PDF's text was very large: make it smaller. Wide tables that still don't fit go on a landscape page, while the other pages stay portrait. Mixed orientation needs a check in Electron's printToPDF.
    - (g) Status Closed set by hand doesn't close the note; Check setup lists it with a Close button: sensible.
    - (h) Footer: yes.
    - (d) Reopen: not answered; with no freeze it only clears the closed mark.
    - DECIDED 2026-10-19 (owner, chat): no freeze. Closing = the PDF(s) to the destination folders + `closed_on` + the hash footer; the note stays live; Reopen clears the mark; hazards left out of the PDF; smaller text, landscape pages only for tables too wide.

- [ ] **Backup-path bug fixed (tests only)**: Manage kit files listed `Extras/kit-backups/<stamp>/…` copies as the kit's scripts (`kitDetectRoles` picked the first `labForm.js` in the vault; the wrong path was then saved in `managed.files[id].path`). Now `kitIsBackup` (`src/kit/paths.ts`) filters detection, `trackedRoles` and `planManaged`. Probably also why the user's real vault kept old scripts: check Manage files… there lists the real paths and Review update… offers the new scripts
- [ ] Leftover copies of kit scripts may sit inside `Extras/kit-backups/<stamp>/…/lab-kit/` (written by a build before 2026-10-04 evening): safe to delete by hand; check Templater's User scripts folder points at the real scripts after pressing Set up
- [ ] **Snippets as plain templates with tokens** (user, 2026-10-09; plan `docs/plans/snippet-token-templates.md`). Phases 1-5 DONE 2026-10-09 (engine `src/kit/snippet-template.ts`, Alt+S via `src/kit/snippet-run.ts`, six snippets shipped as notes, Make my own copy), tests only, not seen in Obsidian (see "Token templates Phase 3-5"). Still open: Phase 3 must use Obsidian's real `parseYaml`, which the tests do not cover; the computed snippets (solution / recipe / raft: molecular weights from the Chemical folder; nmr / gpc / dls / results / method: Methods folder, letters that continue) stay code in `labSnippets.js`; whether they can become templates is the next question (see "Could every snippet be a plain-text token template?").

NOT seen: Esc, a second copy with the same name (` 2`), Review update… ignoring it, phone. User idea: a syntax in the copy so codes and fields are filled when inserted. DECISION NEEDED (options in chat: placeholder tokens in the copy such as the note's code prefix filled at insert time; or a copy that keeps calling the form with the user's own saved defaults; or accept fixed text and say so). Touches `src/kit/snippet-copy.ts`, `src/kit/snippet-copy-ui.ts`, the Alt+S menu template `kits/lab/Templates/Insert snippet.md`
- [ ] **Make my own copy, Phase 3 built 2026-10-09, tests only, not seen in Obsidian** (`src/kit/snippet-copy-ui.ts`, `src/kit/managed-ui.ts` snippet rows; checklist 6i, last line). Check: button, name box, copy in Alt+S, phone. Two things to know: the copy uses the kit's bundled `labSnippets.js`, not an edited one in your vault; the run sets `window.Notice` for its duration because the script uses the global `Notice`. The copy has no `// icon:` / `// desc:` lines, so Alt+S shows it without them (not checked). Phase 4 (split) dropped 2026-10-09, see `docs/plans/snippet-token-templates.md`. Original idea: build the snippet once with empty answers and save it as a plain Markdown file so ordinary users can edit it. Form defaults (NMR solvent, RAFT ratio…) could become plain settings

## Decided (decisions round, 2026-10-04): to build
Order after the store submission: **Kit picker → Chemical database → Analysis methods** (all three built; Analysis methods 2026-10-04, see "Not yet tested"). Each is its own version; plan mode first.
- [ ] **Preview pane** beside the forms (toggle in settings): later, after the store submission
- SDS PDFs → chemical notes: a **separate Python project, not part of Lab Kit** (user's choice). Starter prompt given in the reply of the 2026-10-04 decisions session
- Dropped: Retired files "build kit file from related?" (user: drop the question)

- [ ] **One look app-wide, still theme-friendly (owner, 2026-10-19, chat, after the theme question):** "consider how to harmonise the look app-wide so there is no mismatch, but still keep theme compatibility in case people like them". Today Lab Kit's windows (`.vt-entry`, `.lk-snip-*`, `.lab-kit-win-*` in `styles.css`) use the `--lk-radius-*` shapes (`docs/design-standard.md`) and Obsidian's colour variables, while Obsidian's own Settings, menus and pop-ups keep their own look. Options to put on a feedback page before any plan: (a) Lab Kit pieces only, made consistent with each other, nothing outside them touched (store-safe); (b) an opt-in vault CSS snippet (like `vault-theme.css`) that gives Obsidian's own modals, menus and settings the same corners and spacing, off by default; (c) both. All of them must be checked with the default theme plus 2-3 popular themes (e.g. Minimal, AnuPpuccin) in light and dark through obsidian-check. Not planned yet; ask the owner first (visual change).

Was: FIXED 2026-10-20 (bug-fix batch): Delete now opens the next inbox note in the same leaf before the trash (`sortInbox`, `src/planning/commands.ts`); test with stubs in `tests/planning-commands.test.ts`; not seen in Obsidian, the user confirms with three inbox notes. **USER RESULT 2026-10-17 (chat, Sort inbox): after deleting an item the view goes back to the homepage instead of showing the next inbox item.** Not reproduced, not fixed, not seen. Where to look: `sortInbox` in `src/planning/commands.ts:407`; the loop opens each note with `getLeaf(false).openFile(file)` (line 414) and the delete branch ends with `trashFile(file)` (about line 441). Guess, not checked: trashing the open note closes its leaf and the workspace falls back to the homepage (e.g. a Homepage plugin), and the next `openFile` then opens in another leaf or is hidden. Possible fix: open the next note before trashing, or re-open it in the same leaf after the trash. Needs a test where the stub allows it, else a checklist row.

  - **Computed row references and mode switches**: these snippets build rows in code (row numbers, last-row ranges like `$B$6` / `SUM(D$2:D$n)`, the CTA and initiator rows after the monomers, different column sets for "own targets" / "ratio split" / "total concentration"). Tokens have `each`, `if`, `@seq` and `@i+2`, but no row arithmetic or whole-column-set switching.
  - **A note lookup per list item**: `{{note-number:key.Density}}` reads one property of one named note. Solution prep, Recipe and RAFT need the molecular weight and a `[[link]]` for every item of a list (a link only when the note exists).
  - **Data-driven generation**: NMR / GPC / DLS take columns, defaults and machines from method definitions, and Combined results builds XLOOKUPs across all method tables. Same gap as "Methods folder: could token templates replace it?" below.
  - **A form that reacts to its own answers**: token forms are static; these show defaults that depend on the machine picked.
  - Cost to weigh: every new token is syntax users must learn, and the script snippets work today. Value is in snippets people copy and edit (Make my own copy), not in "all plain text" as a goal.
  - **Suggested first step**: a plan for only Solution prep and Recipe by equivalents (the most used, the closest fit): per-item note properties (MW, link) plus a mode switch. Leave RAFT and the method snippets as code until that works. Needs a decision first: is "all plain text" the goal, or only the snippets people edit?

## Kit update system (spec: kit-update-system-spec.md, kept outside the repo)
Phases 1-5 (core) are built. Left for later:
- [ ] Phase 2 left out on purpose: **view diff** per file (needs the `diff` package), badge "retired", optional "show my other snippets" in the switch, "Open" for snippet files (`.obsidian/snippets` isn't in the vault index, so `getFileByPath` finds nothing, `managed-ui.ts` `render`)
- [ ] Per-file Update / Restore leave `installedKitVersion` alone until no `create` / `fast-forward` file remains (`KitManaged.settleVersion`, `managed-ui.ts`); check the "Update available" banner behaves in Obsidian
- [ ] Phases 3-4 left out on purpose: `pendingConflict` is still always null (status is derived from the plan each time; persist it); no Notice after an update that leaves conflicts; "Update all safe files" never merges (spec); a merged file keeps the "Merged" badge until the next kit write (`ManagedFileState.merged`, `src/kit/managed.ts`); no unchanged context lines around a hunk; the Review window only lists conflicts, resolving is from Manage kit files
- [ ] Merge window (`src/kit/merge-ui.ts`) has no automated test and was **not seen in Obsidian** (check: hunk cards at phone width, Edit textarea, preview, Apply with a script file, Keep all mine then the row shows "Changed by you"). The phone-width fix (`styles.css` ~line 77, container query on `.lab-kit-hunk`) is a best guess
- [ ] Merge edge cases to look at: a user file with no base copy (untracked / base deleted) is always `needs-merge` (`planManaged`); line-based merge of Mermaid / callout blocks in templates can join edits that read badly; merged result equal to the kit text counts as a merge, not "up to date"
- [ ] Phase 5 left out on purpose / to check: no "move" action (a renamed kit file stays where the user has it); nested YAML maps are one block per top-level key; frontmatter after a Templater block is only recognised when the line before `---` is `-%>` (`splitFrontmatter`); a retired file that was never tracked is not listed; Review window's Retired group has no Forget button (Manage kit files does); `scripts/kit-manifest.mjs` `renames` and auto-`removed` **not run** (try on a temp copy of `kits/lab/`); Retired UI and the frontmatter merge **not seen in Obsidian**
- [ ] Backup retention setting (keep the last N update runs) and a Restore-from-backup list (spec section 6). Backups are always made today, folder `<backups>/<ISO timestamp with - for : and .>/<path>`
- [ ] "Forget" for a missing file (`userDeleted` in `ManagedFileState` exists but nothing sets it): `missing` files are listed on every review until recreated
- [ ] Per-file `version` in `kits/lab/kit-manifest.json` = kit version where the content last changed (`scripts/kit-manifest.mjs`); a file edited under an unchanged kit version gets the current version. Always run `npm run kit:manifest` after editing a kit file: `tests/managed.test.ts` fails if the manifest sha256 is stale
- [ ] `rewrite` (`Extras/scripts` -> your scripts folder) makes the written text depend on the Scripts folder setting; if the user changes that folder later, every script reads as changed by the kit (fast-forward) on the next review. Check this is acceptable
- [ ] Review window and Manage files don't show **what a kit change contains** before you keep it (user's words: "revisit how this works, i.e. if someone doesn't update for a long time"). Per-file changelog or the diff view
